# UI Interaction Parity

## Problem

The shared OCaml UI must match the complete application at app.logseq.com, including the main shell, navigation, journals, pages and views, rich-text editing, tasks and properties, search, menus and overlays, settings and graph services. Graph View is excluded. Layout, typography, icons, spacing, interaction, persistence, motion and responsiveness all need evidence on each host. Earlier failures include graph and collaborator menus that do not toggle, input state sampled only at mount, incorrect dialog order and toast lifecycle bypasses. Fixing Settings or Keymap alone cannot complete this objective.

## Proposal

Use app.logseq.com in its disposable Demo graph as the visual and behavioral reference. Follow the application-wide acceptance inventory below, starting from everyday navigation and editing flows and then covering every supported control and state. Preserve rich-text editing. Implement shared behavior once in deps/ui/src and use existing typed LUI primitives wherever they supply the required lifecycle. Preserve alert-dialog outside-press behavior and adapt the native titlebar to macOS. Compare Web and native separately; recording-host tests establish shared behavior but do not prove native motion or visual parity.

Implementation sequence:

1. Write all failing interaction tests for menu toggling, URL editing, dialog order and cleanup, toast identity, and submenu navigation.
2. Verify failures against the current application code and capture browser evidence before changing implementation.
3. Repair shared controls and dialog ownership; derive props with scoped reactive expressions.
4. Use the toast primitive for pause, swipe, duration, and presentation lifecycle, retaining stable keyed identity across model updates.
5. Align component motion styles with the observed production transitions and honor reduced motion.
6. Verify settings dropdowns and nested dialogs, graph/member action menus, view submenus, and notification interactions in the browser. Build and run native shared-host coverage. Record any native renderer limitations explicitly.

## Alternatives considered

### Surface-specific event and timer patches

Adding separate browser and native implementations duplicates behavior and bypasses platform component ownership. It also makes nested focus and dismissal depend on listener registration order.

### Restyle all controls before verifying behavior

The existing semantic stylesheet already contains most production chrome. Behavioral evidence should determine which styles and lifecycle paths need changes.

## Acceptance criteria

- Action-menu triggers toggle, expose their expanded state, retain disabled-item behavior, and close after an action.
- URL editor input and Reset visibility update without remounting; save/reset closes the editor that owns the operation.
- Dialog dismissal follows opening order; nested dismissal leaves the parent intact, and close-all clears all dialog state and request resources.
- Toast updates retain mounted identity, error notifications persist, and transient notifications use platform pause/swipe/dismiss behavior.
- View menus safely handle empty lists and navigate nested submenus without changing the parent action.
- Web motion and focus behavior are compared with app.logseq.com; native behavior and motion claims have corresponding native evidence.
- The UI build, shared-host tests, browser checks, and applicable translation checks pass.
- Compare each accessible page and control state at the same viewport, theme, font, and graph fixture. Save reference, local, and difference screenshots; visual similarity without measured comparison is insufficient.
- Exercise all settings controls, including persistence and reload, and match the switch track, thumb, checked state, focus state, and motion to the reference.
- Verify search opens from a single icon press and graph/action triggers close on the second press.
- Match menu icon placement, readable hover/focus colors, settings navigation, and neutral right-sidebar disclosure headers.
- Restore the correct block and caret or selection after undo/redo of typing, split, merge, indentation, and paste.
- Align heading editing geometry and typography with reading mode; match task status and priority controls.
- Show page-reference autocomplete when the caret enters an existing reference, including keyboard movement and pointer selection.

Next work follows observed application-wide differences, starting with the main shell and everyday navigation/editing flows. Settings work remains one part of this inventory. Screenshots, packages and graph fixtures stay outside commits.

## Verification record

The reference is app.logseq.com. Anonymous Demo graphs are used for repeatable browser comparisons. Current local preview is http://localhost:3001. The initial pull advanced the application branch to a2fa7964bf.

Shared interaction fixes currently cover chronological dialog ownership, nested Escape handling, URL editor reactivity, action-menu state, view submenu navigation, and primitive-owned toast lifetime. Browser evidence exposed renderer defects in cover-popover ownership, overlay alignment, retained toast exits, and trigger outside-press handling. Corresponding LUI changes are in the sibling worktree at /Users/tiensonqin/Codes/projects/lui; its local opam pin and the native Cargo path dependency use that worktree. The renderer changes were published as a4d83c44a30d62396fe8a0366fef451481ae40f6; the application install script now pins that revision.

Initial passing checks: 1,958 Web recording-host checks, 667 native recording-host checks, 87 LUI Rust tests, 51 Web overlay tests, protocol/schema checks, and translation validation. Subsequent changes require rerunning affected checks. The native boot test reproduced and repaired missing idle-window wakeups after OCaml patches.

Browser feedback then reproduced additional failures: duplicate search activation, graph trigger reopen, menu icons after labels, unreadable highlighted text, primary-colored sidebar headings, and a persisted font setting that crashes startup because a hyphenated dataset key is assigned through DOMStringMap. New browser regressions first failed on these application paths. Settings, editing, task controls, and full pixel comparisons remain in progress.

GPUI currently has retained fade entrances/exits, with headless lifecycle coverage. Native card zoom, toast viewport/collapsed-stack geometry, and actual window pixel comparison are not yet verified or fully aligned. The Mac was locked during the native GUI check. Authenticated RTC permission and remote collaboration workflows require account fixtures and are not established by anonymous menu tests.

## Risks

- Native component motion is implemented by the LUI renderer, so CSS parity alone cannot establish GPUI parity.
- RTC permission and collaboration flows require authenticated accounts and remote graph fixtures; the anonymous Demo cannot prove server-backed behavior.
- Timed dismissals and focus restoration must not affect a newer layer or a disposed view.

## Questions

None. The default scope includes Web and GPUI; optional prioritization does not block shared interaction work.

Indentation history now snapshots the caret before asynchronous configuration loading and clears the previous pointer position when restoring keyboard focus. The browser reproductions first inserted the marker at the end after undo and redo; both now restore the original middle-of-text caret.

Keymap search, All/Unset/Disabled filtering, category disclosure, and global folding now update reactively. Category-qualified row identity prevents duplicate commands from breaking reconciliation. Custom binding editing, Search by keys, and Refresh all remain incomplete and are not counted as repaired.

Priority glyphs now belong to the shared vector registry. Its browser regression checks selection and reload persistence. A first test incorrectly assumed that priority always appears in the positioned right-side property region; it now targets the value control within the owning block. Both task status and priority cases passed together. The full 33-case run initially passed 31 cases; status reopening after reload also timed out in that full run, although its isolated rerun passed. The normalized 226 × 266 crop differs by 305 pixels (0.507%), threshold 0.05 with antialiasing excluded. Input caret blinking is suppressed for this captured paint state. The actual GPUI window exposed a startup panic: initial OCaml theme patches accessed ThemeRegistry before deferred component initialization. Component initialization now runs before opening the window, after registering the embedded fonts. Reopening the same isolated native fixture renders the shell without that panic. Cargo build and the linked OCaml boot smoke test pass. This adds actual native startup evidence; native popup rendering and pixel/motion parity still require further investigation.

Latest affected recording tests passed again: 1,966 Web checks, 675 native checks, and two native process tests. The repeated full browser run passed 32 of 33 cases. ## Web editor latency investigation

Pulled refactor/lui to f762cec5ce before reproducing the reported delayed text and caret. Tests used a disposable browser graph on http://localhost:3001; the user's graph was not edited.

The shared structure_pending gate queued ordinary text insertion and pointer events behind every Enter transaction and its canonical page reconciliation, even though the optimistic next block was already mounted. On a page with 500 sibling fixture blocks, twenty Enter/text pairs at 20 ms intervals accumulated up to 1,070 ms of input queue wait. The last text became visible in the DOM after 1,071.4 ms. The captured UI flushes were substantially shorter, so waiting for asynchronous structure completion was the dominant cause.

Enter now mounts and edits its optimistic block immediately while worker transactions remain ordered. Each split captures its own pending text save and cursor history before the next editor opens. Canonical deltas reconcile once the pending split batch catches up; subscription reloads and autosave respect the same boundary. Other structural operations and cross-block navigation retain ordered replay. A failed insertion restores the appropriate preceding editor and replays subsequent user input; a browser test injects a failure in the second pending insertion and verifies that later text and splits survive.

Same-block clicks no longer enqueue a redundant editor transition or leave an old click coordinate for asynchronous refocus. Completion retains the live caret. Another regression showed that measured Home/End navigation compared the derived model with the original unpublished session model and consequently dropped the caret update; publication now checks the session model that owned the event before measurement.

The same 500-block page was reset to its original fixture and measured again with twenty Enter/text pairs at 20 ms intervals. All twenty inserts were visible synchronously after dispatch, with a DOM visibility median of 2.5 ms and maximum of 4.4 ms. No insert entered the structure input queue. The final persisted tree contained 521 blocks and the expected final row. Twenty alternating pointer moves in the same block completed with a median of 2.8 ms and maximum of 4.3 ms; all offsets were correct and caret geometry differed from the target by at most 0.063 px. These are browser event/DOM measurements, not physical display or native GPU latency measurements.

Enable diagnostic printing in the browser console with:

```javascript
window.__editorPerf = true;
window.__navEvents = [];
window.__uiPerf = [];
```

Enable the console's Verbose level to see PERF editor and PERF ui messages. The editor messages include structure duration, queued event kind/depth, and replay wait. UI samples include dispatch, signal flush, DOM application, virtualization, focus, patch counts, and mounted nodes. Logs omit block contents. Set window.__editorPerf = false to stop console printing; window.__uiPerf retains its existing bounded sample buffer.

The new production-browser regressions were observed failing before the behavior changes. Coverage includes text and pointer updates while a real worker request is held, ordered persistence, caret preservation after completion, measured line navigation, and a rejected second insertion. Existing undo/redo, merges, Enter repetition, composition, selection, reference and caret-blink cases are included in the browser verification batch. Shared recording tests pass 1,970 Web checks and 679 native checks, with two passing native process tests. The existing native Rust edits in other sessions were preserved.

The completed browser rerun passed all 82 editor cases. The initial full run had one incorrect expected string after simplifying the held-merge fixture; correcting that fixture expectation and rerunning the complete batch established the passing result. Web build, shared-boundary checks, document validation, and diff whitespace checks passed.

The native batch first addresses shell geometry, toolbar and navigation glyph visibility, titlebar integration, sidebar and popup transitions, scrolling, keyboard focus and dismissal. On macOS, leading navigation controls must coexist with the system traffic lights, controls must never drag the window, and blank header space must remain draggable. Sidebar and content tracks must adapt without clipping labels or shifting the main reading column incorrectly. Production UI controls remain localized and use typed LUI props.

Native acceptance requires actual window evidence for toolbar toggling, search focus/typing/Escape/reopen, both sidebar docks, page navigation, scrolling to both ends, dropdown keyboard and outside-press dismissal, editing and repeated Enter, and live window resize. Motion must be visible on the native backend and honor reduced motion. Interaction latency is compared using identical scripted actions and fixture sizes against the reference implementation. No broad equivalence claim is made before these checks pass.

Rich-text editing remains required: inline formatting, links and references, task status, properties, nested blocks, keyboard commands and IME must remain functional. Reading and editing typography and width must match; replacing the editing surface with a plain-text control is outside the authorized scope.


## Native parity continuation on October 16

The current native host is the OCaml SDL/Cocoa renderer, rather than the historical GPUI host mentioned above. Source and actual runtime evidence take precedence over the older verification records. Use fixed viewport/theme/font/fixture comparisons with reference, local, and difference screenshots. Partial component or recording-host coverage cannot establish application-wide parity.

The next implementation slice repairs the native animation clock. Both native sidebar splits already carry a 300 ms standard resize transition, and the installed LUI host implements split interpolation. The application never advances `Lui_host.tick`, so its animation clock stays uninitialized and split updates take their immediate path. The main loop and the live-expose callback must advance the shared animation clock before applying user-triggered changes, continue frames while motion is active, settle exactly, reverse from displayed geometry, preserve direct drag feedback, and stop repainting when quiet. Reduced-motion preferences must remain effective. First reproduce this through the application's actual frame-preparation helper and real native split layout; then apply the smallest fix and rerun the same path. Popup transitions and the full visual-state inventory remain separate required work.

The native frame helper now advances the LUI clock before input and during live expose, and reads the macOS reduced-motion preference. Its real layout regression failed before the change and passes afterward for a long idle interval, opening, reversal, both collapsed endpoints, immediate drag feedback, reduced motion, and removal without continuing frames. A separate real input regression exposed the missing native split divider capture. LUI commits 5f11c3ad and ff82ad64 add divider dragging and expose its hit/capture state; the application preserves the resize cursor while captured and stops cursor inheritance through presented overlays. Tests cover scaled and nested split tracks, disabled/removed nodes, modal shielding, release outside the gutter, and interruption of active sidebar motion. The complete application release `@all @runtest` and LUI release `@all @runtest @install` passed. Actual visible-window motion and frame-time acceptance remain outstanding.

Native popup, dropdown, and dialog enter/exit motion, placement during resize/scroll, keyboard/focus lifecycle, and matched local/difference captures remain required. The native transition property is still unmapped by the painter and must not be counted as implemented merely because a view emits it.

LUI commit a05db507 adds four-direction collision flipping instead of moving an overflowing popup over its trigger. Its real layout regression failed with a bottom popup at y=120 instead of the available opposite-side y=96. All four directions, preferred-side stability, cross-axis shifting, oversized surfaces, and 1x/2x scaling now pass alongside the full LUI suite.

The signed release was exercised through the actual native window: both sidebar toggles work, the left divider changes pane width, and resizing from 1440 to 1600 logical pixels and back keeps the Settings dialog centered. These observations establish functional geometry, not smooth-motion acceptance. The default Core Animation renderer does not yet report physical presentation timing. ## Nested native surfaces and live scrolling

Actual small-window verification exposed three separate problems after menu overflow was enabled. First, nested popup anchors were refined before their dialog ancestor reached its final placement; a full layout rebuild also registered children before parents. LUI commits 47675b74 and ceb2fad9 resolve ancestor placement first, including an open-tree rebuild and resize at both backing scales. Second, the main application's hit mirror intersected every node with its ancestor scroll clip before the shared hit walk could promote popups. The mirror now keeps full shifted rectangles and leaves clip ownership to the shared walk. Its regression failed for a final menu option outside the dialog scroll clip, while ordinary offscreen scroll content remains excluded.

Third, the actual window reported a 408-device-pixel menu offset while the text remained at its original position. A diagnostic tree dump showed the hit rectangle at y=-400 but the application's paint callback still at y=8. Native scroll getters returned partially applied functions that captured the immutable scroll state at callback creation. LUI commit e30fed0d reads the current state when the node id is supplied. Regressions retain offset and range callbacks before scrolling, exercise both axes, range changes, clamping and reversal, and check the application's cached ancestor-offset callback. The application regression failed before the getter fix and passes afterward. A separate scene assertion requires the final option's emitted marker to match its visible hit rectangle.

Both complete release suites passed: LUI `@all @runtest @install` and the application `@all @runtest`. Existing whole-file formatter failures in the changed LUI window files also reproduce on their parent revision; only the new test body was formatted. No compiler warning was disabled.

The signed release was reopened with an isolated data root and exercised at 1440 by 600 logical pixels. The actual language menu remains within the viewport, scrolls visibly to the final Arabic option, and accepts its click below the dialog's content bounds. Reopening the menu resets its scroll position; selecting English succeeds above the dialog's content bounds. Captures are retained in the external native-ui-parity-artifacts/2026-10-16/scrollfix cohort. This fresh-root run has no graph worker and is not a combined graph-memory or performance acceptance run. Popup entrance/exit motion, exact selected-row anchoring, the measured style differences, and frame-budget acceptance remain outstanding.

## Native surface lifecycle and independent sidebar resizing

LUI commit 82e6b74c on feat/ml-backend adds this slice. Promoted native surfaces now use the host clock for ease-out opacity and 95-to-100-percent scale transitions: 150 ms for menus/popovers and 200 ms for modal surfaces. Dismissal removes live nodes and handlers immediately and retains only an inert display-list snapshot until the exit finishes. Animation-only frames reuse the captured surface without replanning fragments. Reversal starts from the displayed opacity; reduced motion, reused node ids, nested surfaces, exact settling, buffer validity, and idle completion have regression coverage. This is intrinsic native surface presentation; generic CSS-style transition properties remain unmapped.

Right-sidebar dragging exposed a separate animation error. The application compensates the nested left split to preserve its pixel width, but the host animated this dependent correction while applying the pointer-driven outer split immediately. Event batches originating from a split resize now apply all dependent split corrections without interpolation. Programmatic opening and closing retain their existing transitions. The actual host/layout regression failed before this change and passes afterward.

Native divider input also ignored child minimum widths. Pointer movement and release now enforce those widths at both backing scales, including proportional allocation when the window cannot satisfy both minima. Both application sidebars declare a 240-point minimum, and the left inner content retains its expanded width during collapse. An application-level pointer/layout regression failed before these constraints. It then exposed an independent geometry error: native split percentages included the divider width. Both panes now divide the space remaining after the gutter using zero-basis flex growth. Regression coverage includes both collapsed endpoints, intermediate fractions, and 1x/2x layout. Both complete release suites passed after these changes.

Actual-window checks with the default single raster worker exercised both open sidebars, right-divider dragging, the left minimum, Settings opening, and Escape dismissal. The right pane moved from approximately x=1800 to x=2183 device pixels while the left pane retained its width; attempting to narrow the left pane stopped at 480 device pixels. Stable screenshots retain readable labels and toolbar controls. Opening the right sidebar also produced a transient missing-label frame, which remains unresolved. A later submitted-pixel readback matched a complete CPU render byte-for-byte and the stable screenshot was correct; these later samples do not explain the earlier transient.

The guarded application run used an isolated copy of the Demo graph. Its combined GUI/worker physical-memory peak was 81.18 MB. Mixed interaction work-frame median was 9.19 ms and p95 was 36.41 ms; overlay/render p95 was 27.16 ms. This fails both the 8.33 ms target and a claim of smooth dialog motion. Physical presentation timing remains unavailable on this renderer. A fixed 2880x1864 raster-only modal-fade fixture measured approximately 15.49 ms p95 with one worker and 5.79 ms with four, with exact pixel equality against full rendering. The default worker count remains unchanged pending clean end-to-end presentation and memory evidence.

Test process monitoring now normalizes executable paths before identifying the owned worker and also matches its isolated root directory. Earlier GUI-only reports missed workers whose executable contained a `MacOS/../Resources` path. Those reports remain GUI-only evidence. Eight workers left by this session's retired isolated test cohorts were stopped. Screenshots, raw scenes, readbacks, timing samples, test graphs, and packaged binaries remain outside Git and must never be staged with these changes.

Final release validation used serialized Dune jobs (`-j 1`) for both full suites. Running the raster budget test alongside formatter processes exceeded the threshold at 18.91 ms p95; the isolated rerun and serialized suite passed. The fixed budget is a controlled workload gate, not a guarantee under arbitrary competing load. New function bodies were formatted separately; whole-file formatting differs on both the parent revision and the working version of all changed LUI files. This document passes validation; the repository-wide document check still reports 13 preexisting invalid documents.

## Permanent overlay carrier and software rendering phases

Native software rendering now records three separate wall-time phases: raster/setup time outside the upload callbacks, IOSurface upload, and Core Animation submission. Background drawing can overlap upload, so these are partitions of caller elapsed time rather than summed CPU time across domains. Physical presentation timing is still unavailable.

The permanent full-viewport overlay carrier was constructed as a Popover. Its default chrome emitted two full-window shadows even with no popup open, and the retained painter scheduled presentation motion for the carrier itself. The carrier now uses a fixed transparent Overlay; menus and dialogs remain independently promoted surfaces. The actual shell paint regression failed for both idle animation and full-window shadows before this change. Both checks and the complete application release suite pass afterward. A scene exported from the updated window confirms that the full-window shadows are absent.

At 1440x932 logical pixels and 2x scale, four identical Settings open/Escape cycles were compared on isolated copies of the same Demo graph. With four raster workers, removing the carrier's popup chrome reduced raster p95 from 25.75 to 7.34 ms, upload p95 from 16.75 to 5.44 ms, and mixed work-frame p95 from 57.63 to 21.86 ms. The updated cohort's combined GUI/worker physical-memory peak was 93.70 MB. First-dismissal and stable captures contain the same dark-pixel counts in the sidebar and document text regions; this observed path no longer showed missing text. Other transition paths remain unverified. The default software worker count is now four, and an environment override still permits controlled comparisons. A run with no override reported three background raster domains.

These timing runs inadvertently retained debug logging: this executable treats any nonempty LOGSEQ_NATIVE_DEBUG value, including 0, as enabled. Their relative comparison used matching logging settings, but they are not clean production performance acceptance. Logging was removed from replacement test packages; the Mac locked before the corrected comparison could be exercised. Do not claim the 120 FPS target from these results. A separate layer-geometry caching experiment passed pixel/resize tests but did not improve actual submission time (about 2.3 ms p95); that experiment and its diagnostic counters were removed.

The final build received 200 real text-input events in one typing burst and 50 Enter keydowns in approximately five seconds through the native UI. Typing work-frame p95 was 9.10 ms, maximum 16.12 ms; Enter work-frame p95 was 16.95 ms and input-to-paint p95 was 21.79 ms. Those samples also include debug logging. Enter did not immediately terminate the process, but the document became blank. Its retained tree had a 48-device-pixel document body with no page/block content and a main scroll range of zero. The later Escape was followed by process termination; the 200 MB guard did not trigger and no system crash report was found. The exit cause remains unknown. This scenario fails functional acceptance regardless of the measured latency. Preserve the captured tree/scene and investigate virtual row mounting and editor lifecycle before accepting repeated Enter.


## Nested viewport notification ownership

Repeated real-window verification isolated the blank Journals document after 50 Enter presses. An offscreen child block emitted lazy-unmount with a 24-point measured height. Platform.emit_event bubbled that notification through every extension ancestor, so the outer journal-day row also applied the child's height and unmounted its entire subtree. No outer-row unmount was emitted by the viewport sweep. Host viewport notifications now dispatch to their addressed node only; ordinary pointer and keyboard bubbling is unchanged. This also prevents an inner virt-end notification from paginating its enclosing list.

The regression mounts nested native virtual lists with the real extension registry, parent lookup, and event dispatcher. Before the fix, unmounting one child disposed the whole journal day and replaced its height; both assertions failed. Afterward, the parent and sibling survive, the child remounts independently, and nested pagination reaches only its owner. The complete application release @all @runtest suite and the formatted host-test rerun pass.

The signed release was exercised again at 1440x932 logical pixels and 2x scale, with debug logging absent, four raster workers, the same isolated Demo graph, and the combined 200 MB physical-memory guard. A 200-character burst followed by 50 Enter keydowns no longer removes the document. Scrolling reaches the final new block, additional text can be entered there, Escape leaves editing without terminating the process, and scrolling back restores the original top content. After a normal quit and reopen, the final acceptance text and following original blocks remain visible. Captures and raw measurements are external artifacts, excluded from Git.

This run's combined GUI/worker physical-memory peak was 84.87 MB. The typing interval, including initial editor activation, measured work-frame p95 15.14 ms and maximum 65.13 ms; input-to-paint p95 was 14.70 ms. Enter work-frame p95 was 19.41 ms and input-to-paint p95 was 25.32 ms. These results do not meet the full editing or 120 FPS acceptance criteria. The editor also fails to scroll automatically as repeated Enter moves the caret below the viewport; manual scrolling works. This remains a separate follow-up.

A diagnostic parent recorded status 0 for the earlier Escape exit. Source inspection confirms that LUI emits Quit when Escape has no focused node or open surface; the disappearing document had removed that focus. This explains the observed exit without an OCaml exception or system crash report. Changing the idle Escape policy remains outstanding; the active-editor path now exits editing normally.


## Focus reveal and idle Escape

LUI commit c1f56ae5 on feat/ml-backend removes the implicit Quit action from an unfocused Escape. Escape still cancels dragging, dismisses surfaces, and blurs a focused field. An explicit quit input still emits Quit. The changed text/input regression failed before this correction; corresponding platform expectations were updated, and the complete release @all @runtest @install suite passes. Existing whole-file formatter differences also reproduce on the parent versions of all four touched files.

The native editor's focus handshake runs before its newly mounted parent has a measured rectangle. It previously never revealed that parent after layout, so repeated Enter moved the active editor below the window. The host now retains one pending reveal request, waits for painted geometry, reveals that editor once, and requests another paint only when scrolling changed. A blur clears the request. The normal post-paint focus pass completes it; subsequent idle focus checks leave manual scrolling alone. The regression first failed because the offscreen editor remained hidden, then passed for deferred geometry, minimal reveal, one-time behavior, and blur cancellation. The complete application release suite passes with the updated LUI installed.

A fresh signed release with no debug logging was tested on the isolated Demo graph at 1440x932 logical pixels and 2x scale. Two initial Escape presses leave the window open. Fifty Enter presses automatically reveal the final editor, which accepts additional text without manual scrolling. Scrolling back to the document top remains stable; exiting editing and pressing Escape again leaves the application running, and the header menu still opens. Normal application quit works. This verifies focus-following for newly created blocks; caret tracking within a long existing block and the full frame-budget criteria still require separate acceptance.


## Settings geometry, surface chrome, and functional color opacity

A matched 1440x932 reference inspection measured the Settings inner panel at 70 percent of viewport height, a 600-point General content column, 40-point navigation stride, 14/20 language-select typography, 12-point font captions, and a 16-point checkbox. The active theme uses an inset thumbnail ring rather than a filled card. Editor, Advanced, and Features rows use a 28-point minimum label height; longer labels wrap. The modal backdrop uses the active background color at ninety percent opacity.

The existing signed native release reproduced a shorter 572-point Settings card, a zero-size checkbox, a full-card accent fill, inherited 16-point select/global-label text, bold category headings, and missing font-button gaps. Its exported scene also contained a black half-opacity backdrop. These measurements and captures remain outside Git.

The shared Settings controls now declare the measured sizes, spacing, neutral select surface, thumbnail selection ring, and font weights explicitly. The panel uses the measured viewport height and General column limit. Checkboxes reserve a visible hit target. Functional hsl/rgb variable colors now apply their alpha after variable resolution as well as palette resolution; the scrim resolves against the active theme background. The color regression failed for resolved variable opacity and both theme backdrops before the fix.

This exposed two LUI root causes. The dialog constructor accepted surface chrome arguments that runtime protocol validation rejected. LUI commit 8af586fd admits these existing properties in the canonical schema and generated platform schemas, and preserves an existing public declaration in the schema generator. The native layout also ignored border widths entirely and classified width changes as paint-only. Border widths now inset content and invalidate geometry. A real retained-layout regression first failed at a child x-coordinate of 5 instead of 8; it now covers numeric and shorthand borders, fractional widths, repeated changes, zero width, and both backing scales. Exported edges are checked after physical-pixel snapping. Border color remains paint-only. The deterministic demo scene checksums were updated for the corrected control geometry.

Both complete serialized release suites pass: LUI @all @runtest @install and the application @all @runtest. The five component-schema generator tests also pass. The actual Settings view is mounted through the native assembly in a twelve-assertion geometry and typography regression that failed before these changes. Its final dialog measures 1024x654 logical points, with a 1022x652 content panel after native pixel snapping and one-point borders. Newly added test bodies are formatted; whole-file formatter differences also reproduce on all touched parent files.

A signed release package was prepared with debug logging absent and the same isolated Demo graph. The native launch attempt reported a locked Mac and did not start the process; the external watcher recorded no GUI or worker. Therefore there is no post-change visible-window screenshot, click/persistence result, or motion/performance acceptance for this batch. Do not substitute assembly assertions for those missing checks. Select chevron rendering, exact theme thumbnail assets, font-family previews, remaining Settings surfaces, and frame-budget acceptance remain outstanding. Screenshots, graph fixtures, logs, raw scenes, and packaged binaries are excluded from commits.


## Native select indicators and measured font previews

A direct computed-style read of the reference font button descendants corrects the earlier caption measurement: the sample is 14-point bold, while the small caption is 11.2 points at weight 500 with a 16-point line height. The earlier twelve-point caption assertion was inaccurate. The Serif family stack is Lyon-Text, Georgia, ui-serif, serif; the Mono stack is iawriter-mono, Nitti, Menlo, Courier, monospace. Native preview nodes previously inherited Inter for both. The actual Settings assembly regression failed for caption size, family resolution, and weight before these changes. Preview families now use typed properties, captions use the measured size/weight, and Default follows the configured font-family token. These declarations preserve the reference's explicit platform fallback stacks; they do not establish identical font-file availability.

The reference language trigger includes a sixteen-point trailing chevron inside twelve-point horizontal padding. Native paint never requested that image, and the application decoder did not resolve an intrinsic select icon. LUI commit f2938eee adds select/combobox indicator slots through the existing scale-aware image hook, clips long labels before the icon, and carries control opacity onto the image. The native decoder now supplies the cached builtin chevron in the muted foreground color. No new image cache or asset dependency was introduced. The painter regression failed because it never requested an indicator; the application decoder regression independently failed because no icon resolved. Both pass for 1x/2x scale; coverage also checks disabled opacity, narrow controls, visible pixels, cache identity, and ordinary text remaining icon-free. Exact glyph pixel matching remains unverified.

Both serialized release suites pass after the changes. Existing parent formatter differences remain; new painter/test blocks were formatted independently. The generated deterministic scene checksum now includes the select-label clip and padding. A signed release started with the isolated Demo graph and debug logging absent. Its external guard recorded 336 combined GUI/worker samples across 47.3 seconds, with a 57.38 MB physical-memory peak. This is startup-only evidence: native window discovery subsequently reported the Mac locked, preventing interaction, screenshot, persistence, motion, or frame-budget acceptance. The owned processes were stopped. Reference captures, timing/memory samples, test graphs, and release packages remain outside Git.


## Styled native line boxes and editing geometry

The application uses a vendored native text engine that ignored line-height, although the view declares explicit pitches throughout Settings, sidebars, menus, and rich-text editing. Changing a pitch left text measurement, multi-line painting, caret rectangles, and hit testing on the font's natural metrics. The new native regression reproduced each mismatch through the real text engine before implementation.

LUI commit 1ef9c6b7 adds optional explicit line heights to both text backends' layout builders and existing bounded caches. Each layout line owns its styled top, height, and baseline, including signed half-leading for a pitch smaller than the glyph metrics. Measurement, caret, selection, and hit testing consume that same layout. Cache identity includes the pitch for plain text and rich spans, so a style change cannot reuse stale geometry. Zero pitch is accepted; negative and nonfinite heights fail fast. No additional layout cache was introduced.

The application resolves inherited line-height values before requesting those layouts. Unitless strings remain multipliers of the child's computed font size. Absolute lengths and percentages inherit the value computed at their declaring node; numeric wire values are already point lengths. Painting uses the cached line baselines rather than separately accumulating natural font heights. Tests cover pixel/rem/em/percentage values, numeric wire lengths, tight and loose pitches, explicit child overrides, normal metrics, repeated-cache identity, both backing scales, measured height, actual glyph pitch, caret position, and pointer hit testing.

A separate Settings regression failed because its title inherited a 36-point line box instead of the reference's 32 points. The title now declares the measured pitch. The graph-selector label also declares its twenty-point line height directly rather than relying on a stylesheet calculation token. The Settings assembly regression now uses the real text engine for measurement and glyph painting instead of a constant character-width estimate; its geometry and typography assertions pass.

Both complete serialized release suites pass. CoreText's 62 text cases include the new line-box tests; the Pango implementation and signature compile on this host, but Linux-only runtime tests cannot run on macOS. New test/helper blocks were formatted, and whole-file formatter differences also reproduce on the changed parent files.

A signed release with debug logging absent started on the isolated Demo graph. Window discovery still reported the Mac locked, so interactive verification remains unavailable. The running process exported its actual 2880x1864 scene and retained pixel readback before cleanup. The scene contains 79 operations and 123 glyphs; one-worker and four-worker full rendering agree, and the retained readback matches the full rendering byte-for-byte. The sidebar's transparent region is the intended native vibrancy hole; this diagnostic image excludes the system-composited backdrop and is not a screen capture. The combined GUI/worker physical-memory peak was 78.84 MB. These observations establish startup and render consistency only, not visible-window parity, editing latency, or the frame-budget gates. All captures, raw scenes, readbacks, graph data, and packages remain external artifacts.

## Bundled theme images and visible surface borders

Native Settings previously drew simplified theme illustrations. It now uses the same three tracked PNGs as the reference, without modifying those assets. The host decodes them with the existing imagelib dependency, preserves source dimensions, and premultiplies alpha for scene images. Relative paths resolve beneath the bundle's Resources directory; direct development launches declare LOGSEQ_NATIVE_ASSET_DIR. The packaging instructions include the three existing files. Decoded pixels are cached by mounted node and path, then pruned after store revisions remove or replace their owners. Regressions cover orientation, transparency, repeated-paint identity, path changes, and collection after disposal. The synthetic decoder and actual Settings asset assertions failed before implementation.

LUI commit d2f3f177 on feat/ml-backend corrects native image fill to crop centrally while preserving aspect ratio. The previous painter stretched the image, unlike the existing Apple backend. The source-rectangle regression failed before the correction and now covers wide, tall, and equal aspect ratios; fit-mode behavior remains unchanged. The complete serialized release @all @runtest @install suite passes.

An actual Settings paint fixture then exposed transparent dialog, select, and thumbnail-selection borders. The legacy --lui-c-border token is absent, and the attempted --lui-border alias also reaches an undefined native variable. Shared surfaces now use the production hsl(var(--border)) token; the selected thumbnail uses hsl(var(--primary)). Literal HSL parsing already worked and was not changed. Normal CI coverage resolves these properties against the real theme snapshot; the optional exported-scene check also requires visible emitted border fills. Its valid pre-fix failure was a transparent dialog border.

The complete application release @all @runtest suite passes. A second run with the actual fonts and bundled resources decodes each 160x92 image into 58,880 bytes in approximately 1.6 to 1.9 ms. The three retained source images total 172.5 KiB. Full rendering of the 2880x1864 Settings fixture agrees byte-for-byte with one and four raster workers. These are assembly and rendering checks, distinct from screen evidence.

After the Mac was unlocked, the signed release was exercised on the isolated Demo graph with debug logging absent and four raster workers. The default bundle asset path displays all three original thumbnails, visible borders, and the selected-theme ring. Command-comma reopens Settings; Tab focuses the language control and Return opens its menu. Escape dismisses only that menu and preserves Settings; a second Escape closes Settings while leaving the application running. Normal application quit also works. The external guard collected 3,542 samples over 549 seconds, with a combined GUI/worker physical-memory peak of 140.22 MB and no threshold termination.

Native coordinate input still reports a window-discovery error, although screenshots, keyboard input, and native accessibility menu actions work. Consequently this cohort does not verify mouse clicks, scrolling, divider dragging, or resize motion. The language menu's selected-row placement, square select/font-button borders, font weight, description typography, and remaining control styles still differ from the reference. Physical presentation timing and full frame-budget acceptance remain outstanding. Raw screenshots, scenes, timing logs, graph fixtures, and packaged binaries remain outside Git.

## Shared menu height limits

The user requested a component-wide correction rather than a language-menu exception. LUI commit ba07de00 on feat/ml-backend applies the existing Web eighteen-rem default to native dropdown menus, context menus, and popovers declaring menu semantics. At the native sixteen-point base this is 288 logical points. Explicit maximum heights and available-height declarations are supported, with all menu limits bounded by the viewport minus sixteen points. Open menus recompute their bounds on resize. Native menus now scroll and clip intrinsically while retaining their option heights; input and paint share one scrollability rule. Ordinary popovers retain their existing sizing contract. The application's CSS exception that let language menus grow beyond the shared cap was removed; menu popovers receive the same Web limit.

The valid layout regression failed at 932 points instead of 288, and the independent raw-menu interaction regression failed with a zero scroll range. Coverage now includes all three menu representations, smaller and larger explicit limits, available height, shrinking and growing windows, both backing scales, final-option visibility, clipped hit testing, and actual final-option Press delivery. A further regression reproduced stale sizing when an existing popover acquired menu semantics. Role changes now invalidate both the surface and child layout while retaining accessibility invalidation. Removing the role restores ordinary popover sizing. Both complete serialized release suites pass after these changes. Whole-file formatter differences also reproduce on the parent revisions; the changed regression bodies were formatted independently.

The application regression exercises the actual Settings select at 1440x932 and 640x300 logical points with 1x/2x backing scales. Its menu heights are respectively 288 and 284 logical points, and scrolling exposes the final painted and hit-testable option. A new signed release was then bound by its exact application path. In this cohort mouse input works: the visible language menu is bounded, real scrolling reaches Arabic, clicking it updates the trigger and dismisses the menu, reopening resets scrolling, and English can be restored by clicking. The header action menu also uses the shared limit and scrolls to its final Login item. Settings closes by its actual close-button click. The combined GUI/worker physical-memory peak was 102.65 MB across 627 samples over 94.7 seconds; the guard did not trigger and normal quit succeeded.

Keyboard End and ArrowDown did not visibly highlight or scroll the open language menu. That keyboard-routing issue remains a separate acceptance failure; the height, wheel, and pointer results do not establish complete select interaction parity. The native/window captures in the external menu-height cohort are screenshots, while the geometry assertions above are assembly evidence. These files and the packaged test application are excluded from commits. Animation smoothness and frame-budget acceptance remain incomplete.


## Shared native menu keyboard ownership

LUI commit 00d2d87e on feat/ml-backend gives native dropdown menus, context menus, and menu popovers priority over the field focused behind them. Up/Down wrap through enabled options; Home/End and PageUp/PageDown reveal their targets; Return and Space dispatch one Press plus visible logical-coordinate detail. Pointer movement and keyboard navigation share one highlight. Committed text performs case-insensitive prefix lookup. Composition stays out of background fields, while editable search fields inside a menu keep their own text and IME. Menu ownership is synchronized on store changes instead of scanning the tree on pointer motion. Empty permanently mounted menu containers do not own typing; the complete suite initially exposed that distinction through existing textarea composition tests.

The native host now suppresses duplicate document-key dispatch and explicit rich-editor insertion when a component owns the input. Its document PageDown fallback also respects that ownership. Input metrics count native keydowns and text commits before routing so component-owned interactions remain observable. The public menu_input_active query lets hosts keep platform text input enabled for menu prefix lookup, while caret geometry remains limited to editable fields. Actual window testing found the missing SDL text-input activation after the navigation tests passed; a replacement build then received the same letters correctly.

A further lifecycle regression reproduces Space activation followed by menu disposal and its queued text commit. Previously this inserted a space into the background field. The input owner now consumes that one activation commit, and a subsequent ordinary Space remains editable even when the platform omitted the first commit. All regressions exercise the real input state machine. Both complete serialized release suites pass, including the final queued-commit correction, and the formatted input regression rerun passes all 84 cases. Whole-file formatter differences also reproduce on the changed parent revisions. The application assembly test covers End, final-option visibility, exactly one activation, logical detail coordinates, and Home through the actual Settings select at both backing scales and both tested viewport sizes.

Two signed releases were exercised on isolated copies of Demo with debug logging absent and four raster workers. The first verifies End scrolling to Arabic, Return selecting it, Home followed by one Down highlighting the next language, Space restoring English, and header-menu End revealing Login. The replacement verifies that typing t then i locates and selects Tiếng Việt, End still reveals Arabic, and Home plus Space restores English while preserving Settings. Escape closes Settings without quitting. Their combined GUI/worker physical-memory peaks were respectively 98.09 and 94.31 MB over approximately 262 and 210 seconds; neither triggered the 200 MB guard. These are functional interaction samples, not frame-budget acceptance.

The replacement cohort's coordinate clicks did not activate buttons. A diagnostic reopen recorded a header-button press at logical (1383,24), followed by release at (1320,87), outside the button; no Press or click was dispatched. Further pointer diagnosis stopped when native automation reported no available window. This evidence does not identify the source of the mismatched release, and the input implementation does not substitute the press coordinates. Mouse functionality is therefore supported by the earlier height-limit cohort, not reaccepted in this replacement. The diagnostic run enabled logging and is excluded from performance claims. The macOS block context menu observed in the first cohort is AppKit-owned, distinct from the canvas context-menu tests. Tab dismissal, submenu left/right traversal, prefix timing, remaining visual differences, physical presentation timing, and full frame-budget acceptance remain outstanding. All captures, logs, test data, and packages remain outside Git.


## Keymap control geometry and shared icon ownership

The reported Keymap capture reproduced duplicate keyboard glyphs in the search-by-keys button, a shared segmented track behind independent filter pills, missing category bands and disclosures, inherited sixteen-point command text, and compressed rows. The native painter emitted control icons once through default chrome and again through labeled content. LUI commit ffb37fa2 on feat/ml-backend makes button-like kinds use the content path once; the separate number-stepper treatment is preserved. The valid painter regression failed with two images instead of one. It covers buttons, toggles, toggle buttons, and menu triggers, with text, empty text, no text, leading/trailing placement, and both backing scales. The existing menu-item icon regression remains green.

Keymap layout and typography now use typed declarations across hosts. The toolbar has an eight-point field/button gap, a thirty-point button, fourteen-point text, and twenty-two points below the title row. Filter pills retain individual borders without a parent track. Category bands have a themed background, a thirty-point minimum height, fifteen-point text, and a visible fourteen-point disclosure. Command rows use fourteen-point text, thirty-four-point spacing, and eight-point horizontal insets. The old CSS-only control-column padding was removed so it cannot add a second spacing layer on Web.

The final geometry gate exposed a five-device-pixel overlap between the title row and the Keymap pane. The pane's natural content height participated in flex shrinking instead of filling the remaining space. Explicit growth with a zero minimum height on the pane and scroll viewport restores the intended boundary. The existing viewport was already bounded before this correction; the change is not evidence for the cause of the separate memory spike. The actual Settings assembly now verifies these measurements, themed category paint, independent pills, bounded internal scrolling, and exactly one toolbar keyboard image through the production text engine and icon loader.

Both complete serialized release suites pass after the correction. New regression blocks were formatted; whole-file formatting still differs on their parent versions. The application link retains its existing duplicate SDL library warning. A signed release with debug logging absent was exercised at 1440x932 logical points and 2x backing scale. The g s shortcut opens Keymap; typing Undo leaves one matching command; clearing the query restores the list; real wheel input reaches Clear all notifications while the toolbar stays fixed; Escape followed by g s restores the initial list. The captured window shows the corrected icon, category bands, typography, and independent pills. Its combined GUI/worker physical-memory peak was 103.60 MB over approximately 335 seconds, with no guard termination. These checks do not prove physical presentation timing or the full frame-budget gates.

The preceding manual-test cohort crossed the 200 MB guard at 205.59 MB after approximately 126 seconds, including 185.98 MB for the GUI and 19.61 MB for its worker. The replacement cohort used a different interaction sequence, so its lower peak does not establish a memory fix. Search-field icon and inset handling, exact keycap/control widths, focus/hover styling, search-by-keys and reset interactions, custom-binding data, and the remaining application-wide parity and motion checks are still incomplete. Screenshots, scene captures, logs, test data, and packaged applications remain external artifacts and are excluded from commits.

## Keymap continuation: search geometry and functional ownership

The updated signed release was kept open for the requested manual test while source inspection and regression preparation continued. Its external watcher owned the GUI and worker and enforced the combined 200 MB physical-memory limit. After a later live check found no new interaction events over the preceding minute, the application was quit normally before building or running tests again. The watcher confirmed that both owned processes exited without a guard termination.

The shared search-row implementation is used by Keymap, Plugins, and the icon picker. It currently declares the leading icon before the field as an absolutely positioned, automatically sized sibling and supplies the text inset through a DataAttrs style declaration. The native text placement reads typed padding properties and otherwise uses a ten-point symmetric field inset; it does not read that declaration. This explains the source-level inset mismatch. The missing-icon investigation must also check its retained bounds and paint ordering, rather than assuming that reserving text space makes the image visible. The new actual Settings assembly assertions require exactly one positive-size search image within the field, no later opaque surface covering it, a twenty-eight-point text/caret origin, and an input hit target under the icon. These assertions are written but have not been executed during the manual session. The existing fixture covers the real application assembly and text engine at 2x; the next verification must also cover 1x, narrow widths, nonempty input, and the other shared callers before claiming a component-wide correction.

Reference source was inspected at upstream revision 32570a626aba699c089f20f9c940a6cb127c3add, in frontend/components/shortcut.cljs and shortcut.css. This is source evidence, not a substitute for observing the deployed reference. The source declares twenty-eight-point insets on both sides of the Keymap search field, including space for its query-clear button. Other shared callers require their own measured right inset; the current pad_left parameter is insufficient to describe that distinction. The reference also uses a fifteen-point search glyph. The deployed toolbar measurement remains authoritative where its spacing differs from this source revision.

The remaining functional acceptance is broader than appearance:

| Control or state | Reference behavior from source | Current application evidence | Required verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Search by keys | Opens an end-aligned anchored recording popover, clears the text query, and restores trigger focus on dismissal | The native/shared button has no Press handler and no recording model | Open, record modified chords and unmodified sequences, filter actual bindings, dismiss without editing or executing a background shortcut, reopen with the current filter |
| Recording | Modified chords commit immediately; unmodified sequences commit after 400 ms and contain at most five strokes; Backspace removes the pending last stroke or clears a committed filter; Escape closes the recorder | No corresponding state machine is connected to Keymap | Deterministic timing and lifecycle tests, trailing text-commit ownership, cleanup after Settings closes, and visible native interaction |
| Query clear | Appears only for a nonempty query, clears it, and returns focus to the search field | Shared search-row trailing controls are supported, but Keymap supplies none | Actual query input, clear press, focus/caret restoration, and repeated reopen |
| Refresh | Rereads shortcut data without resetting bindings | The icon has no Press handler and Keymap reads an embedded default snapshot | Refresh after a real binding update and prove that the configured binding is preserved |
| Shortcut rows | Open a binding editor with recording, conflict feedback, remove, reset, and persistence | Rows render embedded bindings without a customization action | Edit through the real dispatcher/configuration source; verify execution, conflicts, reopen, and application restart |
| Query/filter result list | Deduplicates commands across categories when flattened; supports keyboard navigation and visible no-results state | Current filtering preserves category rows, uses a label substring, and has no connected custom-binding source | Compare queries by description and action ID, filter counts, empty results, keyboard focus/scrolling, and configured custom/disabled/unset states |

The next implementation must use a shared typed geometry and input-ownership path. It must not add a field-only Web style exception, global recording listeners without disposal, fake refresh through a visual rebuild, or a second shortcut configuration that the editor does not execute. Full application parity, motion, and frame-budget acceptance remain open.

The source-level shortcut data audit also found a concrete mismatch between presentation and dispatch. Editor_keys.chord_table derives sequences from Commands_data.table, including sidebar/clear as mod+c mod+c, search/re-index as mod+c mod+s, and ui/highlight-recent-blocks as mod+c mod+r. Keymap_data stores each of those sequences as two separate combo records with empty normalized data strings. A key-filter implementation over those empty strings would fail even if the recording popup worked. The correction must derive display and matching from the same actual stroke sequence that dispatch consumes, retaining the distinction between alternative bindings and consecutive strokes. The current chord table is lazy and static, so future persisted updates must also invalidate or replace the dispatch source; merely changing the Keymap model would leave execution stale. This evidence defines the integration boundary for the next functional regressions and does not establish that configurable dispatch is implemented.

The actual Settings regression failed because a later opaque input surface covered the already present search image. ## Modal paint and shared CSS channel values

The native resolver performs variable substitution inside color functions instead of stripping their wrappers or guessing foreground aliases. Shared muted-foreground fallbacks now explicitly wrap channel values in hsl(). Named dialogs explicitly use the shared background token, matching the reference even when the card token differs in dark mode.

The native substitution and shared-snapshot regressions failed before these corrections. ## Keystroke filter recording

The reference opens an end-aligned, 288-point popover below Search by keys. Opening clears the text query. Modified keys commit immediately; plain strokes accumulate for 400 milliseconds with a five-stroke limit. Backspace edits an accumulating sequence or clears a committed recording. Escape dismisses only the recorder and preserves its filter; clear actions remove it. Global command execution must remain suppressed during recording. Current shared controls have no press handler. The host's ordinary component key handling precedes document delivery, so a recorder also needs scoped keyboard ownership before controls consume Tab, Return, or Escape. Implement that ownership in the common application input path and honor it in native routing; keep recording state, timers, filtering, placement, and controls shared across hosts.

The shared recorder now implements these interactions, including modifier-only and composition exclusion, pending sequence deletion, committed sequence replacement, and prefix filtering. Three multi-stroke bindings now carry their actual sequence data, so the first modified stroke also finds those commands. Recording acquires exclusive keyboard ownership for its mounted scope. Both entry points install capture before ordinary UI listeners; the native host bypasses ordinary component, editor, clipboard-shortcut, focus-restoration, and page-scroll routing while recording. Disposal releases ownership and cancels the single pending timeout. No idle interval was introduced.

Runtime verification exposed three further defects before their corrections. A custom Clear button without an accessible label caused the Web store to reject a patch; the dynamic-button contract regression now catches it. Binding metadata reused as node identifiers created duplicate Tab IDs; it now uses a data attribute. Adding the outside clear button changed the popup's preceding-sibling anchor and erased its gap. One shared anchor group now encloses the trigger and clear control, and native retained-layout coverage checks that recording preserves the gap. These changes remain shared component structure rather than separate platform geometry.

The actual browser also recorded Shift+/ as shift+?, finding no Toggle help command. Recording now uses the same normalization and folded-Shift inference as editor command dispatch, extracted without changing the editor mappings. Regression coverage includes shifted punctuation, the plus key, named keycaps, native folded Shift, scope disposal, timer cancellation, and the native document-event path. The native fixture records Meta+C and finds four prefix matches. The browser driver did not deliver that chord into either the local or reference recorder; this does not establish physical Meta+C acceptance.

The final serialized release build and native suite pass, and the rebuilt Web suite reports 2112 checks with zero failures. Browser-script syntax and changed-file hardcoded-text lint pass. The exact exported browser assertions pass against the real local app for opening from a text query, Meta+K, Shift+/, Tab, Enter, Escape, focus return, stable trigger identity, clear, and closing/reopening Settings. Both empty and recorded popovers retain right alignment and a measured five-point gap, including the four-point offset and surface border. The final measured Web surface is 286 by 101 points. No warning or error appeared in the final interaction cohort. Screenshot recording-accepted.png and the corresponding geometry/lifecycle JSON remain in the external artifact directory. The legacy browser runner was syntax-checked; its shared assertions were executed through the supported browser driver.

Native document-event and retained-layout fixtures do not prove physical SDL keyboard delivery, native pixels, or animation smoothness. Long named-key sequence wrapping and non-US modifier-key layouts remain unverified. Refresh/reset, binding customization and persistence, remaining command-data differences, full style and motion parity, native physical-window acceptance, and frame-budget gates remain open. Sharing a component tree and typed props is the layout contract; Web CSS leakage and backend defaults must still be checked independently against that contract. The earlier list-item regression failed on native as well as Web, so an apparently correct native view is not evidence that the implementations already agree.

## Clean native motion profiling

The external release packaging helper now removes both diagnostic logging and tree dumping by default. A guarded five-cycle Settings open/Escape workload, driven through the actual window at 1440 by 932 logical pixels, produced 186 work frames with p50 9.96 ms and p95 29.03 ms. Raster p95 was 8.99 ms, upload 5.40 ms, and compositor submission 1.91 ms. Opening also incurred layout p95 11.48 ms and a scene-build maximum of 21.34 ms. These phase percentiles are separate distributions and must not be added as a reconstructed frame. The external ring samples retain the complete 186-frame cohort; the longer request/idle-loop histories exceed their 240-slot capacity and do not represent the same full cohort.

A separate twelve-second CPU sample during six more actual open/Escape cycles identifies translucent span blending as the largest active leaf: 347 sampled stacks versus 118 for shadows, 105 for images and 62 for software tile upload. The backdrop and modal repeatedly blend long runs of identical destination pixels. First exercise exact scalar-equivalence checks across changing/repeated runs and the existing full Retina modal workload with a single worker against the 8.33 ms render budget; then test a span-local reuse of the previous input/output pair without changing rounding, boundaries, alpha semantics or retained frame pixels. Compare the same native five-cycle workload afterward. This is one measured bottleneck, not a replacement for the remaining layout, upload, physical-presentation, or editing gates.

The span now reuses only its previous exact input/output pair. Scalar-equivalence checks cover all nontrivial alpha values with repeated and changing runs, multiple rows and odd boundaries. Allocation remains bounded on both flat and varying backgrounds: 4,952 and 5,440 bytes for a 512-by-128 render. Both complete dependency release builds and all 48 raster tests pass. A more complex run-scanning/bulk-copy experiment was slower and was removed.

The exploratory single-worker modal median improved from 13.50 to 8.84 ms but still misses 8.33 ms. Requiring the entire existing multi-worker fixture to meet that budget on one worker is not the application's configured contract, so the exploratory registration is not a permanent acceptance test. The production four-worker test retains its original 8.33 ms gate unchanged. Three alternating runs of separately built baseline and candidate executables measured baseline p50 5.02/4.06/4.09 ms and p95 9.40/4.68/4.77 ms, versus candidate p50 3.18/3.39/2.99 ms and p95 4.82/3.68/3.30 ms. All candidate runs preserve exact output equality against complete rendering. These controlled raster results still require the actual-window comparison and do not establish end-to-end 120 FPS.

Dependency commit 89180d36 on feat/ml-backend contains the simple span change and regressions. After installing it, the complete integrated application release build and native tests pass. A second actual-window five-cycle cohort starts from a settled closed dialog so no warmup close is mixed into the one-second metric exports. Raster p50/p95 changes from 4.80/8.99 ms to 3.97/7.29 ms; work-frame p50/p95 changes from 9.96/29.03 ms to 9.45/25.62 ms. Input latency p95 remains approximately 55 ms. Layout p95 is 12.38 ms and scene-build maximum 20.43 ms after the change, so opening responsiveness remains a measured bottleneck. ### 2026-10-17 native picker lifecycle and remount cost

LUI commits 38c19ef4 and 4047e270 initialize the enabled selected menu row on opening, reveal its layout without replacing the editor focus session, and align native select labels over their trigger labels when viewport bounds permit. Layout regressions cover unselected, first, middle, last and disabled selections, both backing scales, resize, rebuilding, edge collision and explicit point placement. Actual computer-driven acceptance confirms initial highlight, Down, End, prefix lookup, scrolling, Escape, reopening, Enter activation and resized-window anchoring. These checks do not establish global control or animation parity.

The prior CPU sample includes PNG parsing during Settings opening. The actual release image hook decodes each of the three bundled 160-by-92 thumbnails in approximately 2.0 to 2.1 ms, totaling approximately 6.2 ms for 176,640 decoded pixel bytes. Its cache belongs to node identities and is pruned when the dialog unmounts, so each new dialog pays that cost again. The real-hook regression first fails because remounting the same path produces a different image resource.

Keep mounted-node ownership and add a resource cache bounded to 1 MiB of pixel data and 32 entries. Oversized images are not retained by the resource cache. Keys include the resolved path and a digest of freshly read compressed content, so modifying a file at the same path is detected on remount. The small bounded sequence keeps newest resources on insertion; it adds no idle work. Regressions verify unchanged decoding, dimensions and premultiplied pixels, same-resource reuse after disposal/remount, changed paths and same-path replacement, pixel-budget eviction, oversized-image release and bounded path history. ### 2026-10-17 contiguous software upload classification

A fresh twelve-second CPU sample during real Settings open/close cycles no longer shows repeated PNG decoding on the warm main-thread path. A local immutable width allows the production compiler to vectorize the inner loop. The array occupies four stack bytes per backing-pixel column and is released after each upload; retained surfaces, pixel ownership, alpha, alignment and copying remain unchanged.

### Keymap category transitions

The live reference retains all nine category headings, including the empty Plugins group. Its global category control collapses all whenever any category is open, and expands all only when every category is collapsed. Shared code instead treats any collapsed category as a request to expand, while the query projection discards empty categories even without a query. Search results also hide the two category tools in the live reference; retain those tools when only a status filter is active. The shared query projection now retains empty headings only for an empty query, the global control expands only when every category is collapsed, and scoped conditional mounting hides category tools during text search. ### Native surface transition contract

Shared popover recipes declare 120 ms opacity and transform transitions with cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0, 1). Native retained surfaces ignore that property, use a fixed 150 ms ease-out, and derive zoom from opacity. This prevents independent opacity/transform durations, delays, disabled transitions and correct reversal timing. First exercise these through the real native host with deterministic clocks, including named/cubic/step easing, declaration precedence, partial reversals, reduced motion, detached shells and idle completion. Consume the existing typed Transition property instead of adding a per-application animation channel. Keep each active track's timing and update future transitions from live properties; explicit declarations without opacity or transform must snap those channels. Preserve existing defaults when no transition is supplied. This corrects declared cross-platform behavior; reference-specific timing/origin policy, grouped-opacity compositing, Web exit cleanup and physical smoothness remain separate acceptance requirements.

Implemented in LUI `6ae9ce04` on `feat/ml-backend`. The five new deterministic motion cases initially fail against fixed native timing; all 13 motion cases now pass, including independent tracks, 13 easing examples, overshoot, explicit zero-duration channels, delays, declaration precedence, repeated reversal, live recipe refresh, detached-shell release and reduced motion. Shared transition tokenization preserves functional easing arguments. Unitless zero times are rejected by the shared property validator: actual Chrome `CSS.supports` rejects `opacity 0` and accepts `opacity 0ms`, consistent with the [CSS time definition](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#time). Complete serialized LUI/app release builds and native suites pass, as do 2,129 Web checks.

Computer-driven acceptance repeats five recorder open/Escape cycles at the same 1440-by-932 logical Retina window with four raster workers. The candidate's combined GUI/worker physical peak is 111.25 MB, its 200 MB guard never triggers, and both owned processes are stopped. Actual input still records Command-Z, filters to Undo and closes only the recorder with Escape; clearing the filter and closing Settings work.
