### Web surface motion ownership and exit completion

Implemented in LUI `5c0123fe` on `feat/ml-backend`.

Live reference measurements confirm 150 ms entry animation for the toolbar menu and Keymap recorder, both using a centered 95-percent zoom with directional slide classes. Settings uses a centered 200 ms ease-out dialog entry. The current shared recorder declaration remains 120 ms cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0, 1), the generic Web dialog remains 100 ms, and Web popup scale/origin still differ from native defaults. These visual policies remain unfinished; the following change repairs the lifecycle needed to express them correctly.

The real Web fixture reproduces two independent causes. Transition properties are written onto the outer positioning container, although opacity and scale animate on its inner popup. The visible popup therefore keeps its default 120 ms recipe. Separately, duplicated exit helpers finish on the first transition end/cancel or a fixed 130/170/470 ms timer; modal cleanup watches the backdrop without waiting for the card. An opacity ending before transform, or a shorter backdrop, removes visible content prematurely. The first wall-clock probe also exposes timing-sensitive assertions, so the committed regression controls actual browser Animation tracks directly instead of estimating completion with sleeps.

Use one DOM helper to collect finite animations belonging to the specified surface elements, wait for each finished promise, and treat cancellation as settlement of only that track. Modal exits include both backdrop and card. Descendant and infinite decorative animations cannot retain the shell; zero-motion and reduced-motion paths complete immediately. Existing layer tokens continue to protect modal/menu/popover reopening. The implementation follows the browser's [getAnimations](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/getAnimations) and [Animation.finished](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Animation/finished) lifecycle rather than adding another duration parser or timeout. Replace the menu/overlay timer helpers and remove the unused copy from the positioning module. Apply and remove the typed Transition property on the visible popup; the positioning container keeps its geometry role.

The controlled real-browser regression contains five scenarios and thirteen assertions: independent opacity/transform completion, delayed card versus backdrop, zero duration, one cancelled track with another pending, same-node reopening and an infinite child animation. Restoring the original six backend source files and rebuilding produces eight failing assertions; restoring the candidate produces thirteen passes. Browser warnings/errors are empty. Existing select/combobox tests now finish real animations rather than dispatching a synthetic opacity event. The legacy full browser runner is syntax-checked, while its exact new audit-button flow is executed through the supported computer driver. The complete serialized LUI/app release builds and native suites pass, all 56 non-browser Web library checks pass after rebuilding the previously stale stylesheet output, and the integrated Web suite passes 2,131 checks. No generated CSS, screenshots, packages or test graph data are committed.

Actual integrated app checks verify the declaration on the visible recorder and no transition on its outer positioner, open/Escape/reopen, Command-Z filtering to Undo, filter clearing to 123 rows, Settings dismissal, restored main-interface interaction and reopening. Full tooltip/toast interruption coverage, reference motion timing/origin/slide, grouped-opacity compositing and physical 120 FPS acceptance remain open. This change establishes correct declaration routing and exit ownership; it does not claim new native latency or frame-rate gains.

### Context-menu motion and tooltip exit ownership

Implemented in LUI `0d0556e9` on `feat/ml-backend`.

The separate Web ContextMenu kind does not use the popup entrance/exit lifecycle: it sets data-open directly, removes that attribute on close and immediately releases layer presence. ### Native compound SVG contours

### Distinguish unavailable presentation metrics and exclusive overlay work

The default native software renderer submits Core Animation surfaces, but only the Metal path supplies displayed-frame counters and presentation intervals. The metrics record initializes FPS to zero, so both the header and JSON incorrectly report a measured zero on the software path. Apple documents presentedTime as the onscreen time of a Metal drawable; Core Animation transactions group render-tree updates and their completion blocks concern animations. A transaction commit or flush cannot be substituted for a displayed-frame acknowledgment. References: https://developer.apple.com/documentation/metal/mtldrawable/presentedtime and https://developer.apple.com/documentation/quartzcore/catransaction.

Represent the unmeasured rate explicitly as None. Export JSON null until an observed display interval establishes a value. Show n/a FPS in the developer header and unavailable in the presentation rows. An observed interval with no additional displayed frames still reports a measured zero. The existing counters and presentation-based acceptance gates retain their meaning; no render-attempt or display-refresh counter is relabeled as actual FPS.

The same runtime investigation exposes a second attribution error: the overlay timer encloses the downstream renderer and subtracts only GPU submission time. Software rasterization, surface upload and Core Animation submission are therefore counted again as overlay work. Extract that existing timing boundary into a small testable helper, reproduce the error, and end overlay measurement before calling the renderer. Total render timing and scene cleanup remain around the complete operation. There is no new timer thread, cache, sampling state or rendering strategy.

The rate regression first fails because an unobserved rate exports zero. It now covers initial state, render work without presentation feedback, incomplete sampling intervals, observed 60 FPS and an observed zero. The exclusive-timing regression fails with 2 ms of preparation plus 75 ms of downstream rendering; after repair it records only 2 ms. Renderer and preparation exceptions remain visible, and a preparation failure never submits a frame. Full serialized release builds, native suites, js_app and 2,181 Web checks pass. Changed-file hardcoded-text lint and whitespace checks pass.

Actual native verification repeats three sidebar close/open cycles, the graph menu, shrinking and expanding the window, 43 bytes of input and ten Enter keys. Exported counters confirm all input and Enter events. The developer panel opens and closes, shows unavailable presentation measurements and survives restarting the same disposable app with the edited text and new blocks intact. The repaired first-run metrics report overlay p50/p95 around 0.33/1.20 ms, surface-upload p50 around 3.74 ms and active-frame p95 around 40.92 ms. These are diagnostic observations, not proof of meeting either frame budget. The earlier instrumented baseline and the later panel-open candidate are not a controlled performance comparison.

An external 60-second Animation Hitches recording reaches its time limit but stalls while stopping. A process sample finds the recording tool waiting in authorized_ktrace_stop/XPC and consuming about 2.6 GB itself. Stop that owned tool rather than treating its incomplete trace as presentation evidence or changing system permissions. No usable system presentation timeline is obtained, so actual software presentation FPS remains unverified.

The hot-loop overlay-cache check reads the existing optional rate directly; formatting stays in the painted developer label. A final serialized release build and separate native launch verify that path, the panel and JSON null with zero presentation samples. This avoids introducing repeated number formatting into the idle poll.

The protected baseline application cohort runs 129.91 seconds and peaks at 72,975,728 combined physical bytes. The candidate runs 99.82 seconds and peaks at 79,431,000 bytes; its restart runs 22.05 seconds and peaks at 70,370,600 bytes. Final cache-path verification runs 30.83 seconds and peaks at 71,271,768 bytes. None reaches the external 200 MB limit, and all eight owned GUI/worker process IDs are independently confirmed absent. The recording tool is also confirmed stopped. Screenshots, traces, samples, metrics logs, binaries and graph edits remain outside Git. Continue with targeted CPU attribution in rasterization, surface upload and submission, long-list scrolling and the remaining full-interface parity inventory; the full goal and physical 120/60 FPS gates remain open.

## Menu highlight and surface parity on October 17

Continue the complete application inventory with the header, sidebar and context menus. At a 1440 by 932 viewport, the reference page menu is 256 points wide and 370.914 points high. Rows have a 32-point pitch, 6/8-point padding and a 4-point corner radius. The card has 4-point padding and a 1-point border. The highlighted reference row is RGB 232/232/232 with dark text. Its local `--muted` override resolves to gray step 05; reading the root variable alone gives a different color.

The native runtime reproduces an accent-blue highlight with inverted text, no card content inset on the point-positioned page menu, and a 288-point height cap despite enough window space. Scrolling reaches the remaining actions, so this is a visual sizing mismatch rather than evidence that those actions are missing. Shared menu rows previously depended on stylesheet-only state decoration, while native fell through to toolkit defaults. Point-positioned page and sidebar menus also bypassed the shared card recipe.

Write native paint/layout regressions before repair. Pointer and keyboard states fail in both light and dark modes, and the eleven-row menu fails the available-height requirement. A separate point-menu case first fails its five-point content inset. Checking the measured gray-step token also rejects the superficially neutral global `--muted` value. ## Native SVG curve and packed-path parity

The native graph selector renders database-plus/database-import as crossed, heavy shapes even though the actual Web application and reference carry identical SVG path data. A same-source raster fixture reproduces the defect without application layout, theme or caching differences. The framework cubic flattener visits the second half before the first while prepending to a reversed point accumulator; the final reversal therefore changes traversal between curve segments. Packed relative path commands also keep the origin from the first segment instead of advancing with each implicit repetition. SVG2 path semantics define each explicit or implicit drawing command as a segment relative to its current point: https://www.w3.org/TR/SVG2/paths.html#PathData.

Framework commit 3670332b on feat/ml-backend corrects traversal and samples the origin per relative segment. It adds no icon-specific SVG replacements or application rendering branch. Exact Bezier/subdivision stroke-and-fill comparisons cover cubic and quadratic curves at 24/36/48/96 pixels. Packed/expanded comparisons cover relative move, line, horizontal, vertical, cubic/smooth cubic, quadratic/smooth quadratic and arc forms, with an absolute-move control at three sizes. The corrected exact-subdivision test is rerun against the original implementation and fails before the production fix is restored; packed relative moves also fail. All thirteen SVG tests then pass. Only curves.svg and icon.svg change among eleven existing fixture hashes. Their new shapes are independently checked against the browser before updating those two expectations. The complete serialized framework release/native suites and eighty-five Web tests pass; modified-file formatting remains nonzero on both current and unchanged parent versions, while the added test block is formatted independently.

The external same-source fixture retains browser SVG, native-before and native-after images at 18/36/48/96 pixels. Final measurement uses device scale one, integer element origins and raw RGB samples from captures of all three presentations in the same browser output. This avoids comparing different backing scales or differently transformed color encodings. There is no antialias exclusion; the differing-pixel threshold is an absolute RGB difference greater than thirteen. At 36 pixels, database-plus mean channel error falls from 29.49 to 3.39 and database-import from 29.85 to 4.17. At 96 pixels the curve fixture falls from 18.24 to 0.58, with differing pixels falling from 1,499 to 200 of 9,216. The filled icon falls from 56.49 to 0.49, with differing pixels falling from 3,292 to 150. The unchanged arc-only layout icon remains a control. These results establish corrected geometry with residual rasterization differences, not pixel identity. Evidence and the exact final comparison are outside Git under svg-curves-20261018.

After the published framework is installed, the complete application release/native suites and all 2,496 JavaScript checks pass. Protected signed native acceptance opens the graph selector, closes it with a second trigger press, enters Settings/Keymap, changes to classic dark, reopens the graph menu, restores classic light and resizes from 1080 by 600 to the observed 901 by 501 with the menu open. Database curves retain their correct form and inherited foreground in these states. The earlier missing-text case does not recur here and remains unproven as fixed. The guard records 551 paired samples over 94.23 seconds and peaks at 104,482,160 combined physical bytes without triggering. It reaches terminal completion; exact owned GUI/worker PIDs 75930/75967 are independently absent before shell verification. Native evidence is outside Git under native-svg-curves-final-20261018. ## Retained popup placement and exact border-box measurement

The graph-menu horizontal difference has a concrete reference-side cause. Current master creates a one-pixel virtual anchor; Base UI 1.6.0 creates an invisible arrow element for transform-origin calculation and applies its default five-point arrow padding. Floating UI adjusts alignment when that padding cannot fit the narrow anchor. This explains the measured start/end shifts; do not treat 4.5 as an arbitrary application layout constant. The official component contract documents arrow padding at https://base-ui.com/react/components/menu. No compensating graph-menu offset is added.

A separate real-renderer audit exposes functional positioning defects shared by Web popovers and dropdown menus. Changing side/alignment/offset without resizing the anchor does not request a new placement. Removed alignment and offset properties also leave their DOM styles behind. The expanded existing surface fixture first fails thirty-two of sixty-nine checks. Framework c0782823 on feat/ml-backend queues anchor and point placement once per affected popup in the existing batch epilogue, after final styles and split geometry. It clears removed alignment/offset state and preserves authored tooltip alignment during side changes. An intermediate actual-renderer run then isolates twenty-six remaining failures: the old measure adds one pixel to rounded dimensions, yielding one-point shifts and a 1.5-point shift for a 96.5-point card. Measure the undecorated, untransformed positioner border box instead. Tooltip surfaces that own their positioner use untransformed offset dimensions. No per-call-site correction is introduced.

All sixty-nine real-renderer checks pass after the final change, with no warnings/errors in the recorded final audit time window. Existing real-renderer menu/tooltip interruption and surface-exit audits also pass. The published framework is installed before the complete application release/native suites, 2,496 JavaScript checks and production Web bundle pass. This change affects the Web renderer; no new physical-native-motion claim is made.

Close/reopen retains correct placement, and closing the recorder preserves the Settings dialog. No shortcut definitions are changed. Both application/reference dialogs are closed and temporary viewport overrides are cleared afterward. Evidence remains outside Git under popup-geometry-20261018.

The reference recorder still measures 290 points wide and starts at y=216 versus the application's 288-point card at y=220. Its virtual-anchor policy also shifts its right edge beyond the trigger. These are application recipe/reference differences distinct from the corrected renderer error. Nested popup movement ordering, cover/anchored mode changes, native/Web default-gap and viewport-edge policy, unsupported centered-anchor cleanup, the full interface/theme inventory and physical FPS remain open. The goal remains application-wide parity rather than completion of this popup slice.


## Search footer actions and shared clipboard ownership

Actual page-creation acceptance exposes an application-wide interaction defect in the portable search view: every footer hint button has an empty press callback. Clicking Create leaves the palette open and the route unchanged, while the same action creates a page in the reference. Browser error/warning logs are empty. The shared view already has working keyboard action owners; remove the empty callbacks and route primary, sidebar and copy actions through current highlighted-state operations. Read the selection when pressed rather than capturing the item used to mount the footer: same-variant highlight changes deliberately retain its nodes. Preserve move-block mode through the existing primary action. Remove the inert action for an absent selection and the block-reference copy action on file results.

The new production-view regression first fails fourteen checks covering page/tag creation, command dispatch, filters, current-selection navigation, retained button identity, block resolution, sidebar opening, copy and move mode. The primary actions then pass. Actual Web copying exposes a second shared defect: the legacy clipboard helper inserts a hidden textarea under the body and invokes execCommand while the modal owns focus. The modal closes but both browser clipboard reads return an empty string without a warning/error. A second regression requires the browser clipboard service rather than that selection-dependent compatibility path and fails three copy checks. Delete the hidden-textarea/execCommand implementation and write through the existing browser Clipboard API. Native keeps its existing system clipboard service. Both footer clicks and the platform copy chord use one portable selected-reference operation, writing UUID references for pages and blocks.

The complete release/native suites, all 2,511 JavaScript checks, production Web bundle and hardcoded-string lint pass. Actual Web acceptance now creates Parity comparison 2026-10-18 through the footer, enters its first block, accepts cafe-accent/Chinese/emoji and bold markup, and preserves the rendered content after reload. Copy ref writes the exact selected page UUID reference; Open in sidebar opens the same content on the right. Final browser error/warning logs are empty. Evidence is outside Git under cmdk-footer-20261018.

Protected signed native release acceptance uses the same page title in an owned graph fixture. Footer Create enters the first editor; Unicode input and Enter produce a rendered bold block and a following empty block. Copy ref is verified by pasting the system clipboard back into search, yielding the created page UUID reference. Footer Open in sidebar opens the content. A process restart followed by footer Open confirms that both blocks and rich text persist. The first guard records 582 paired samples over 81.80 seconds, peaking at 92,669,392 combined physical bytes. Restart acceptance records 196 paired samples over 30.41 seconds, peaking at 73,499,968 bytes. Neither triggers the 200,000,000-byte limit. Watchers reach terminal completion, and both exact owned pairs, 32668/32704 and 33856/33887, are independently absent before subsequent shell checks. Evidence remains outside Git under native-cmdk-footer-20261018. These short functional runs do not establish long-duration memory stability or presentation FPS; the complete interface/theme inventory and physical FPS gates remain open.

## Portable selects and asynchronous popup publication

Actual native Filter opens blank options because its old HTML extension rows carry CSS-only layout. Their actual production-view regressions fail before removing those invalid properties; portable labels and layout replace the DOM attributes.

No per-popup timer or platform branch is added.

## Preserve native application icon namespaces

Following the application-wide resource cleanup, inspect the actual native raster path before adding appearance channels. Host_icons.svg_of removes app: and then prioritizes the framework builtin table. The shared view reference alone therefore does not prove native asset parity.

Keep the complete wire name in the native SVG cache and prefix installed application overrides once at the registry boundary. Application names resolve only against the shared override set and bundled Tabler data; bare names resolve only against the framework table. Delete the cross-family fallback rather than aliasing individual glyphs. No extra resource table or eager Tabler conversion is added. Registry replacement clears source and raster caches as before.

Native production-boundary regressions exercise search, check, settings, plus and chevron-right in both application-first and framework-first lookup order. They compare both namespaces with their owning sources and compare the actual Host_icons.image_of pixels with the canonical SVG rasterization at scale two. They also cover an application override colliding with a builtin and a missing application name that exists only as a framework alias. The unchanged implementation fails canonical search resolution before the fix. The complete serialized release/native build and test suite then passes. Native bootstrap remains below its existing allocation/payload limits, and warm cached SVG lookup measures 16 bytes per call.

A newly signed native build opens Pages, filters to the Parity page and restores all four rows, opens the application menu and Settings, switches to dark, opens Keymap and restores light before dismissing Settings. The checked stderr contains no application exception. Protected acceptance records 655 paired samples over 102.28 seconds with a combined physical-memory peak of 105,727,440 bytes. The watcher reaches terminal completion and exact owned PIDs 94486/94803 are independently absent before further checks. Captures and memory data remain outside Git under native-icon-namespaces-20261018.

## Shared native truncation tokens

The common application caption recipe already requests text-overflow:ellipsis. The native Core Text path instead calls CTLineCreateTruncatedLine with a null truncation token. [Apple documents](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coretext/ctlinecreatetruncatedline%28_%3A_%3A_%3A_%3A%29?language=objc) that a null token cuts text without adding an ellipsis. Fix that shared renderer rather than adding application labels or per-control clipping rules.

Framework 009a4082 on feat/ml-backend supplies an attributed U+2026 token using the same font and paragraph attributes. Its synthetic run maps to the source UTF-8 end rather than exposing offsets into the token string. All Core Text object ownership paths release the additional objects. The new regression first fails the visible-token assertion against the unchanged implementation. It then covers start, middle and end truncation, both direction flags, system/Helvetica/monospace fonts, ASCII and accented/combining/Chinese/emoji text, valid retained byte boundaries, fitting labels and widths too small for a token. All sixty-three text tests pass.

The first complete framework run fails the unchanged pure-raster modal fade budget while text tests pass: p50 14.14 ms and p95 52.39 ms. That raster target does not link the Core Text path. Its isolated unchanged executable passes at p50 3.38 ms and p95 4.08 ms, and the repeated complete serialized release/native check passes. No raster implementation or threshold is changed. These measurements are test evidence, not a claim about physical application FPS.

Install the committed framework revision into the existing switch and rebuild the application. The complete application release/native build and test alias passes. Protected signed native acceptance switches to Graph switch acceptance 2026-10-18 and captures its visible trailing ellipsis. Switching back to Demo also confirms the Recent caption renders its ellipsis through the same renderer. An attempted sidebar drag leaves the width unchanged, so resize behavior is not accepted by this run. The guard records 182 paired samples over 25.25 seconds with a 61,293,984-byte combined physical peak and no limit trigger. It reaches terminal completion; exact owned PIDs 49055/49496 are independently absent before subsequent checks. This short functional run does not establish long-duration memory stability. Captures remain outside Git under native-ellipsis-20261018, and RED/GREEN text and isolated raster logs are under ellipsis-coretext-20261018.

This closes visible native ellipsis for the common truncation path. Shared icon brightness/hover interpolation, exact popup anchors and motion, full theme/font and interface coverage, resize acceptance and physical FPS gates remain open. The complete interface parity goal is unchanged.

No document listener, polling loop or application handler is introduced.

## Property result colors and action semantics

The actual reference chooser starts with its first result highlighted. The initial run exposes an actual native parser failure on `hsl(0 0 95%)`. Modern HSL permits numeric saturation and lightness channels; legacy comma syntax requires percentages ([CSS Color 4](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/#the-hsl-notation)). Framework b4fa97ac fixes that parser boundary and rejects malformed or nonfinite channels and explicit alpha. Full release framework tests pass before installation.

## Load native locale resources before constructing views

Parsing, successful-load caching and ordinary lookups remain shared. Use the same native resource-path function for images and dictionaries. Load and check initialization before Lui_app.create_with_extensions, where root views are already constructed, rather than waiting until app.start. Delete both obsolete native loadDictFile C stubs. Known-locale file/fetch/parse errors propagate; unknown locales and missing individual keys retain their existing English fallback.

The browser theme contract first rejects the generic Popover background and six-point radius. Rich preview math has rendered KaTeX nodes and the thirty-two-point image loads successfully. Search updates retain the actual input identifier and both reference tables. Pointer activation and Enter each open one expanded shortcut panel, close the help palette, and reopen a previously closed dock.

A second runtime comparison catches a shared sidebar collapse defect: a collapsed local item still reserves one hundred points, while the reference reserves thirty-two. Add browser and native geometry assertions, reduce the collapsed minimum to its header height, and hide the body through the typed Display property. Both reproductions pass afterwards. The complete release/native suite and 4859 shared JavaScript checks pass; hardcoded-string lint and the production bundle pass. The native fixture uses the live reducer model and actual text measurement, checks eighteen table rows at both scales, verifies one outer scroll surface, retained search input, collapsed height and repeated entry. The standalone browser runner contains the same contracts but is not claimed as executed; actual browser assertions run through the authorized browser tools. Browser warning/error logs are empty.

The physical desktop becomes available during final acceptance. Refresh and sign the existing isolated release package, launch it through the native app tool, and verify the actual help button, menu item activation, shortcut body, right-sidebar scrolling, collapse, closing and reopening from the help menu. Native screenshots under help-native-*-20261019 capture the menu and reopened body. The GUI RSS sample after scrolling is 115.3 MiB. Restore Journals, close the test sidebar item and quit the native app; process inspection confirms no native GUI remains. Physical 120/60 FPS remains unproven: the sampled FPS field is null, so the visible n/a label is not a performance acceptance result. Native rich-preview pixel accuracy beyond the visible first examples is not claimed.

This completes the shortcut entry/body repair, not the full Help or whole-interface parity objective. The Handbook and in-app Bug report flows are still absent and remain explicit required work. A measured generic table-layout gap also remains: at the same 464-point width, Web gives both columns 231.5 points with table-layout:fixed, whereas the reference uses auto layout with trigger columns 259.8046875/203.1953125 and syntax columns 316.953125/146.046875. ## Share intrinsic table columns and reference content flow

The observed native table gap comes from independent flex rows. Framework e4e517f6 on feat/ml-backend measures each dirty table's cells, assigns one column vector across its rows and reuses those measurements on unchanged layout and viewport resize. Nested tables are prepared inside out and constrained outside in. Retained tests cover aligned rows, changing content, explicit minimum bounds, empty/ragged tables, nested tables, resizing and measurement reuse. Table cells expose the already admitted typography props directly, removing the application's nested Column wrappers. These wrappers also changed Web intrinsic widths and line boxes. The Web table uses auto layout instead of fixed equal columns; no application-specific column ratios are introduced.

The content comparison identifies independent inline-flow defects. Framework ac69aeaa leaves plain links inline while icon-bearing links retain flex alignment. The reference Learn more link explicitly stays on one line. Use typed nowrap rather than changing column proportions. Render the compact display-math example through the shared inline math preview, right-align the block image through a typed row, and use the reference system monospace stack, zero Pre margins/padding, explicit line-height units and wrapping highlighted code. The existing highlight script needs its existing dependency's Obsidian stylesheet; import it and preserve the reference transparent background in dark mode. Framework b0e2cbbb admits pre-wrap through the shared protocol and Apple/Android validation. The Apple admission regression fails before the change and passes afterward; Android runtime rendering is not tested.

Translation validation catches an ordinary OCaml Unicode arrow literal before release. Replace it with the existing portable JavaScript string form and add a shared renderer assertion. Do not add a runtime encoding guess or decoder. The final actual Web label is Learn more followed by the correct right arrow.

A screenshot exposes white header text when hovering a read-only light-theme table. The generic row hover selector applies an interactive accent foreground to every row. Framework be9b67e4 gates hover feedback on cells with press actions in both native paint and Web. The native regression fails before the repair, then checks read-only neutrality, interactive feedback and removal of the action. The browser foreground changes from the failing white value to the reference rgb(23,23,23); dark mode retains the reference rgb(164,181,182). Selected-row styling remains explicit.

The complete framework release build/native suite and all 126 compiled Web tests pass. After installing that revision, the complete application release/native suite and all 4860 shared JavaScript checks pass. The twelve stylesheet contracts pass. Translation validation, changed-file hardcoded-string lint, production CSS and Vite bundling pass. Expected rejected-save and render-data teardown diagnostics remain in the integration tests. Formatting enforcement is not claimed because the framework has no formatter configuration.

Execute the checked-in pure measurement/assertion functions on the production application and reference through computer automation at 1280 by 720, left dock 246 and right dock 512. Both content boxes are 480 points and both tables 464. Trigger columns are 259.6875/203.3125 locally against 259.8046875/203.1953125 in the reference. Syntax columns are 317.015625/145.984375 against 316.953125/146.046875. Every row height matches, including the fractional math and raw-code lines. Light and dark pass the same geometry and hover checks. Both code previews are highlighted block elements with pre-wrap; light background/foreground are rgb(40,43,46)/rgb(224,226,228), dark background is transparent with the same foreground. Browser errors are empty; the reference emits its existing initial OPFS storage-pressure warning. Restore both appearances and close the test docks. The standalone browser runner is not executed; its pure contracts are executed through the authorized browser tools.

The actual-font native application fixture now uses its offered viewport instead of a fixed 512-point root. An added width assertion first exposes that fixture error. Remove the fixed test width, then verify actual table width changes and column alignment at 512/400/900 points and scales one/two. This establishes native column sharing and responsive available width, not complete CSS min-content or collapsed-border parity. Those remain required work, alongside standalone native preview paint, whole-interface coverage, animation and physical 120/60 FPS acceptance. Screenshots and measurements stay outside Git under help-table-*-20261019. Keep the full objective active.

## Measure native Journals startup from process birth

Add an opt-in boot trace after frame completion/submission that records readiness, journal count, root-block count and scene-operation count. The module initialization timer excludes launcher/dynamic-loader time, so pair its absolute timestamp with libproc process birth. Launch the signed release bundle through the native app tool and verify Journals visually. With a newly spawned worker and warm file caches, the window is created at 1.61 seconds and the Journals startup frame is submitted at 2.29 seconds. A later launch reusing the running worker creates the window at 0.92 seconds, first submits Journals content at 1.78 seconds and completes its related startup frames at 1.92 seconds. These are individual observed launches of the existing Demo graph, not a cold-start percentile or compositor scanout measurement.

Leave the application running for manual testing. It is the startup-measurement release binary; subsequent table content-flow and hover repairs have been built and tested but are not loaded into that running process. Do not interrupt manual testing to replace it. Preserve startup reports, logs and screenshots outside Git under journal-startup-*-20261019. Startup evidence does not close the remaining UI, native motion or frame-rate requirements.


## Replace floating Help press containers with one shared button

The actual Web trigger is a DIV with no role, label or keyboard tab stop. Its press action belongs to a nested DIV. The native shell separately owns another press-enabled Box and a differently sized glyph. This prevents ordinary keyboard entry and duplicates the control recipe across runtimes. Record the DOM admission before implementation and add shared/native mounted regressions. The native assertion fails on the non-button trigger; the shared drive reports two expected behavior failures for button semantics and trigger activation. An initial shared reproduction also reaches the existing Melange raw-backtrace limitation after trying to inspect an absent popup; separate activation from the existing menu-body test so the final RED run records the two actual behavior failures without that secondary exception.

Create one shared labelled standard button with the existing thirty-two-point circle, thirty-point canonical glyph, semantic colors and hover opacity. Both shells call it, retaining only their fixed/overlay placement. Remove their press-enabled wrapper trees and duplicate icons. Keep the live anchor identifier on the actual button. Add help/toggle-menu under the Help owner with English and Simplified Chinese text; command descriptions and Handbook titles are different textual roles and are not reused as this accessible action label.

The complete application release/native suites and all 4864 shared JavaScript checks pass. Translation validation, hardcoded-string lint, dictionary formatting and production Vite bundling pass. The native fixture verifies the actual standard button kind, translated accessibility label and existing Help/sidebar flow; it does not operate the user's running window. Update the browser regression to locate the labelled button instead of clicking a generic class selector.

Reload the actual production Web application and activate the button with Enter. The same Help geometry contract passes: trigger 32 by 32, menu 260 by 401, eight-point gap and matching right edges at the measured viewport. Escape removes the popup after its exit transition and leaves focus on the trigger. Space reopens it; Tab enters Handbook, the next Tab reaches Keyboard shortcuts, and Enter opens the expanded shortcut sidebar and closes the palette. The existing shortcut-body assertion passes. Preserve the DOM RED/GREEN reports and visible keyboard-focus screenshot outside Git under help-trigger-*-20261019. Browser warning/error logs are checked and the test dock is closed after acceptance. The standalone browser runner is not claimed as executed.

The native application remains running for manual testing on its earlier startup-measurement binary. Do not restart it to load this change. Standalone native keyboard/paint, Handbook/Bug report flows, native table-border/min-content parity, the complete surface inventory and animation/FPS acceptance remain required. Keep the whole-interface goal active.
